That fits a Lambda function URL exactly: any container serves any request. Clients still on 2025-03-26 to 2025-11-25 open with `initialize`; those revisions let a server issue no session ID, so the endpoint is **dual-era and sessionless**: `initialize` answers with the client's version (or 2025-11-25) and no session, later requests are plain JSON-RPC. Every answer is one `application/json` response (allowed by both eras); GET and DELETE get 405, as 2026-07-28 says for older clients' stream and session requests. Server-to-client requests on an SSE stream (2025 elicitation) can't work statelessly and aren't offered; 2026-07-28 replaces them with multi round-trip results, which part 3 uses.

**Why not a library.** Effect 4.0.5 ships `effect/ai/McpServer` with a request-scoped 2026-07-28 transport, but only for sessionless revisions: its 2025 runtimes keep sessions in memory, which breaks across Lambda containers. The official TypeScript SDK (2.3.1) is promise- and zod-based, so every tool would be an `Effect.runPromise` edge inside the Lambda, against "Effect at the edges only". The surface needed is small (`initialize`, `ping`, `server/discover`, `tools/list`, `tools/call`), so `apps/api/src/mcp` implements it with Schema at the boundary, and the official SDK client is the independent check in the stack test.

**Authorization.** A resource server for `<origin>/mcp`: a request needs a live `alias_oat_` access token bound to that resource (RFC 8707), of a grant that still exists and a user who still exists. Without one: 401 with `WWW-Authenticate: Bearer resource_metadata="<origin>/.well-known/oauth-protected-resource/mcp", scope="…"` (plus `error="invalid_token"` for a bad one), and RFC 9728 metadata at that URL and at the root (`authorization_servers` = the issuer, `scopes_supported` = what the tools need). Personal API tokens are refused: MCP servers must accept only tokens issued for them. A tool whose scope the token lacks gets 403 `error="insufficient_scope"` with the union of the granted scopes and the needed one, which the SDK turns into step-up authorization. Unknown tokens count against the client address like API tokens. Browsers' `Origin` must be the app's own (DNS rebinding, Streamable HTTP "Security").

**Tools** (`Tools.ts`): `list_aliases` (with the domains new aliases can go on), `create_alias` (random on a shared domain, or an address on the user's subdomain or verified custom domain), `set_alias_active`, `list_contacts`, `block_contact`. Each names one scope and describes input and output with Effect Schemas, sent as JSON Schema (`inputSchema`, `outputSchema`) and used to decode the arguments and encode `structuredContent`. They call the same functions as the HTTP handlers (`aliases.ts` and `contacts.ts` now export them), so validation, ownership and webhooks are shared. API errors and bad arguments come back as `isError` results for the model; an unknown tool is `-32602`. Lists are cacheable (`ttlMs`, `cacheScope: "private"`). Temp-mail tools (create a temp address, wait for its OTP) wait for §20.22's API.

**Prompt injection.** Text in results is attacker-controlled more often than it looks: contact names and addresses come from mail headers, and aliases created on the fly (§20.8) have local parts the sender chose. The text content of every tool result is the summary, then the data as JSON between `<untrusted-mail-data>` markers, with every `<` escaped as `\u003c` inside, so no value can close the block; the server instructions and every tool description say to treat that block as data. No tool reads mail content today; the send tool (part 3) is confirm-gated so an injected instruction can't send mail by itself.

**Tests.** API handler (`apps/api/test/mcp.test.ts`): the 401 challenge and resource metadata, invalid and personal tokens, `server/discover` and `tools/list` with their envelopes, every tool as its user, failures as tool errors, the injection fence on a hostile contact name, insufficient scope, header and version validation (`-32020`, `-32022` with the supported list, Base64 `Mcp-Name`), the legacy era (`initialize`, `notifications/initialized` → 202, `ping`, no session ID), 405s, batches and responses refused, a foreign `Origin`, and a disconnected app stopped at once. Stack (`e2e`): the official MCP TypeScript SDK client (`@modelcontextprotocol/client` 2.3.1) gets the 401, discovers both metadata documents, registers, sends the browser to `/oauth/authorize` with PKCE, the signed-in user consents to two scopes, the client checks `iss` and redeems the code, then connects as a 2026-07-28 client (negotiated through `server/discover`) and as a 2025-11-25 one, and creates and lists an alias the web API then shows.

**Connecting clients locally** is in the `alias-dev-stack` skill (Claude Code over HTTP; Claude Desktop through a local stdio bridge, since its remote connectors run in Anthropic's cloud). Neither was driven by hand here: completing the consent needs a passkey in a real browser.

#### Confirm-gated send (part 3)

**What the spec offers** (2026-07-28). Server-to-client requests are gone; a tool that needs the user returns an `InputRequiredResult` (`resultType: "input_required"`) with `inputRequests` (here one `elicitation/create`) and an opaque `requestState`, and the client retries the call with `inputResponses` and the same `requestState` (multi round-trip requests, MRTR). Elicitation has a **form** mode (the client collects an answer) and a **URL** mode (the client shows its user a link and its host, gets consent, opens it; the outcome is learned on the retry). A form "yes" is answered by the client, which a prompt-injected or careless client could fake; a URL opens our own page, where only the signed-in user can act. Servers must not request modes a client didn't declare, and must treat `requestState` as attacker-controlled.

**Design: drafts only the user sends.** The MCP side can only draft; nothing a token does sends mail.

1. `send_mail` (`mail:send`) takes an alias of the user, a contact of that alias (by id), a subject (≤ 128) and a plain-text body (≤ 20,000). Only contacts: the people the user can already reply to through reverse aliases, so a hijacked assistant can't mail strangers. The alias must be active, the contact not blocked. It stores a **draft** (`OutboxRepo`, `USER#<userId>` / `DRAFT#<id>`, pending for an hour, kept a day) and sends nothing.
2. A 2026-07-28 client that declared `elicitation.url` gets `input_required` with a URL elicitation to `<origin>/outbox/<draftId>` and `requestState` = the draft id. Any other client (legacy, or modern without URL mode) gets the draft with `approveAt`, the same link, to hand the user, and `get_send_status` tells the outcome later.
3. The web page (`/outbox/$draftId`, signed in, §20.16 components) shows from, to, subject and the exact text, and names the connected app that drafted it. **Send** and **Discard** are session-only endpoints (`OutboxGroup`: `GET /v1/outbox/:id`, `POST …/send`, `POST …/discard`); no token reaches them, `mail:send` included.
4. On the retry, the server reads the draft named by `requestState` only if it is the caller's and holds exactly the retried arguments (tampering can only fail the call); `sent`, `discarded` or `expired` finish the call; a client-side decline discards the draft; still pending asks again.

**Sending.** Send re-checks the alias (active, same alias) and contact (still there, not blocked), counts one against the user's daily allowance (`SENDS#<date>`, the `Quota` helper; 50 drafts until §20.33 made it 100 for replies, composed mail and drafts together), refuses an address on the SES suppression list (§20.33), moves the draft out of `pending` with a conditional update (so two clicks or two tabs send once), then sends through `Transport` from the alias to the contact's address, tagged `reply` (a message to a contact: bounces suppress, complaints mark nothing, as for replies, §20.11). If SES refuses or is unavailable the draft goes back to pending. The message is `Mail.compose` (core): `From` the alias, a fresh `Message-ID` on the alias's domain, the subject one sanitized header line (encoded-words when not ASCII), the body UTF-8 in base64, so neither can inject header lines. The API Lambda gets the same `SendFromAliasDomains` statement as the inbound one.

**Tests.** Core (`Compose.test.ts`: headers, a hostile subject and body, encoded-word subjects, line lengths), repository (`OutboxRepo` on both engines: per-user drafts and TTL, one send under a three-way race, lapse after the hour, reopen only after a send, the daily count; `AccountRepo`: erasure takes drafts and counts), API handler (`apps/api/test/outbox.test.ts`: drafted but unsent, sent from the web once with the message checked on fakecloud, tokens with `mail:send` and the OAuth token refused at the send endpoint, MRTR ask → accept while pending asks again → sent, decline discards, `requestState` with other arguments, of nobody's draft and of another user's draft refused, blocked contact and deactivated alias, lapse, the daily cap, discard, the scope), stack (`e2e`: Grace mails the alias over SMTP and becomes a contact; the official SDK client, connected with `contacts:read mail:send`, lists contacts and calls `send_mail`; its URL-elicitation handler, standing in for the user, sends the draft through the web API and accepts; the SDK retries with `requestState` and gets `sent`; Grace's mail comes from the alias and doesn't name the owner's mailbox).

**Not done.** Mail to new addresses from MCP (the web app composes to any address since §20.33; `send_mail` stays contacts-only) and attachments. HTML bodies. A temp-mail tool that waits for an OTP waits for §20.22.

### 20.25 Threat model and security review (2026-10-22)

A STRIDE pass over every trust boundary of `main` at `3235a30` (everything up to §20.24), then a review of the code against it: three independent reviewers (inbound and reply path; web, API, OAuth and MCP; fetchers, stored data, infra and supply chain), each finding checked again in the code, and the slow-input findings measured. **Two High findings and seven cheap Medium/Low ones are fixed** in a stack of three PRs (below); everything else is the prioritized list at the end, with what each needs from the owner.

**Assets, in the order an attacker wants them.** (1) Sending as someone's alias (the reply path, drafts), which is identity theft toward their contacts; (2) the owner's real address behind an alias, the service's reason to exist; (3) stored mail (inbox mode, temp inboxes) and contacts (third parties' addresses); (4) credentials: session cookies, personal and OAuth tokens, webhook secrets, the SSM keyrings (reverse tokens, image proxy, inbox KEKs); (5) the SES account's reputation, which every user shares (§20.13); (6) availability of forwarding; (7) the AWS bill.

**Adversaries.** Anyone who can send mail (the whole internet, no account); a signed-up user (accounts are free and passkey-only, §20.15); a malicious or prompt-injected MCP client or CLI holding a scoped token; a sender-controlled web host (images, webhook receivers, client metadata documents); someone with read access to the table, a backup or logs; a compromised dependency or CI action.

**Not in scope.** AWS itself, Cloudflare DNS (a hijacked zone takes the domain, as for any mail service), the owner's own mailbox provider, and the user's devices.

#### Trust boundaries (STRIDE)

| Boundary                                                                              | Threats (S/T/R/I/D/E)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | Controls in the code                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | Residual risk (list below)             |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------- |
| **Internet → SES → inbound Lambda** (SMTP, receipt rule, S3 spool, Lambda event)      | **S** spoofed senders and forged headers; **T** crafted MIME (deep nesting, millions of parts, regex-hostile text); **I** an alias revealing the owner's address in forwarded mail; **D** mail bombs, loops, slow-to-process mail, contact-item floods; **E** making our SES send to arbitrary addresses (open relay), creating aliases on someone's subdomain | Event parsed at the boundary (`SesEvent.decode`); recipients only the owner's verified default recipient; SESv2 `Destination` explicit, so headers never pick recipients; spam/virus `FAIL` dropped; 25 MiB cap before reading (`HeadObject`); loop marker, hop count, `Auto-Submitted`; hourly cap per alias; on-the-fly/catch-all opt-in with a daily cap; idempotent delivery claims; header rewrite with sanitized display names; spool `aws:SourceAccount` + `aws:SourceArn`; **now:** every pass over a message is linear in its size and the MIME walk is bounded (SR-1)                                                                                                                                                                                               | SR-8, SR-10, SR-24                     |
| **Reply path** (owner → reverse alias → contact)                                      | **S** a stranger sending as the alias; **T** a tampered or replayed reverse token; **I** the owner's address, name, key or provider leaking to the contact; **E** reaching non-contacts                                                                                                                                                                        | 80-bit HMAC token over alias address + contact id, constant-time compare, key rotation (§20.14); contact must exist on that alias and not be blocked; SES DKIM **and** DMARC `PASS`; the `From` address must be a verified recipient (full address, lowercased); quoted-text scrubbing (§20.10); fresh `Message-ID`; client/network fields stripped; **now:** exactly one `From` field of one mailbox (SR-3), `Autocrypt`/`OpenPGP`/`Resent-*` stripped (SR-4); since §20.33 also first messages to contacts the owner added (their send-to address), with the same checks, nothing to SES-suppressed addresses and at most 100 owner sends a day                                                                                                                             | SR-11, SR-21                           |
| **Forward path → owner's mailbox**                                                    | **I** trackers and remote images revealing the reader; **T** headers that make the owner's client answer around the reverse alias                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | Tracker removal, optional image proxy (§20.19), PGP/MIME fail-closed (§20.21); **now:** `Mail-Followup-To`, `Mail-Reply-To`, `Errors-To`, `X-Confirm-Reading-To` removed (SR-4)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | SR-22                                  |
| **Browser → web app / API** (passkeys, `__Host-` session cookie)                      | **S** account takeover, session theft; **T** CSRF; **R** unattributed changes; **I** IDOR on aliases, contacts, inboxes, domains; **D** sign-up and challenge floods; **E** a stolen cookie erasing the account                                                                                                                                                | `@simplewebauthn/server` with exact origin and RP ID, user verification required, single-use 5-minute challenges bound to the ceremony (erasure: to the user); 256-bit session tokens stored as SHA-256, `__Host-; Secure; HttpOnly; SameSite=Strict`; no CORS headers; Effect HttpApi decodes every payload; ownership checked in every handler and repository condition (reviewed endpoint by endpoint); erasure needs a fresh passkey assertion                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | SR-6, SR-9, SR-13, SR-14, SR-25, SR-26 |
| **Tokens, OAuth 2.1, device login, MCP**                                              | **S** token theft and replay; **E** scope escalation, tokens minting tokens, an OAuth access token used on `/v1`; **T** `requestState` tampering, PKCE downgrade, redirect manipulation; **I** prompt injection through mail-derived text; **E** a prompt-injected client sending mail                                                                         | Tokens are 256 random bits with scanner-friendly prefixes, stored as SHA-256; endpoints without a scope annotation are session-only (tokens, webhooks, erasure, device approval, consent, outbox send); OAuth: `S256` only, exact redirect URIs (loopback port only), single-use codes (reuse revokes the grant), rotating refresh tokens with reuse detection, scopes only narrow, RFC 8707 resource binding, `iss`; MCP accepts only `alias_oat_` tokens bound to `/mcp`, checks `Origin`; tool data fenced with `<` escaped; `send_mail` only drafts to existing contacts and only a signed-in session sends; failure counters per source address; **now:** client metadata documents read with a byte cap (SR-5), summaries outside the fence quote no stored text (SR-6) | SR-9, SR-15, SR-16, SR-17              |
| **Outbound fetchers** (image proxy, webhook delivery, client metadata documents, DoH) | **E** SSRF to the metadata service, the Lambda runtime API (127.0.0.1:9001) or private ranges; DNS rebinding; **D** slow or endless origins; **I** leaking the reader's IP or cookies                                                                                                                                                                          | One address rule (`WebhookUrl.isAllowedAddress`) for all three; checked inside the socket's own DNS lookup (every address, both families), IP literals before; redirects never followed (webhooks, CIMD) or re-checked (proxy, 3 hops); deadlines and byte caps; the proxy signs URLs (128-bit HMAC), sends fixed headers, serves only magic-number-checked raster types with `nosniff` and a sandbox CSP, has its own role and a reserved concurrency of 10 (now 20, with per-owner and per-host fetch budgets, §20.29); DoH answers parsed with a schema over HTTPS; **now:** `localhost.` and 3fff::/20, 5f00::/16 refused (SR-7)                                                                                                                                          | — (SR-12 fixed, §20.29)                |
| **Stored data and keys** (DynamoDB, S3, SSM, logs)                                    | **I** a table, backup or log dump revealing mail, addresses or credentials; **T** moved or swapped ciphertext; **R** erasure undone by a restore                                                                                                                                                                                                               | Sessions, tokens, OAuth secrets and device codes stored as SHA-256; inbox bodies and summaries sealed with AES-256-GCM per-message keys wrapped by SSM-held KEKs, AAD binding alias, message and part; keyrings only from `SecureString`, ≥ 32-byte secrets, errors never echo values; spool deleted after processing, 1-day lifecycle; PITR 7 days with an erasure journal; logs carry reasons and message ids, not addresses (checked: schema decode errors name the path and expected type, not the value); **now:** every Lambda's logs expire after 14 days, the spool is TLS-only, the table has deletion protection (SR-7)                                                                                                                                             | SR-18, SR-27                           |
| **Infra and IAM** (Alchemy, roles, resource policies)                                 | **E** a bug or compromised function acting beyond its role; confused deputy through service principals; **T** deleting or reconfiguring the service's own SES identities                                                                                                                                                                                       | Per-function roles with single-item DynamoDB actions where possible; the image proxy can read its keys and nothing else; resource policies for SES→S3, SES→Lambda, SNS→Lambda, CloudWatch→SNS and CloudFront OAC carry `aws:SourceArn`/`aws:SourceAccount` or `sourceArn`; explicit `Deny` on mutating the service's identities; **now:** temp-mail domains can't be claimed as custom domains (SR-2), the `Deny` covers them, sending from them is denied, and confirmations name this account's identity                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | SR-8, SR-23, SR-28, SR-29              |
| **Supply chain** (npm, mise tools, CI, downloads)                                     | **T** a malicious package version, a moved action tag, a swapped download                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | `pnpm-lock.yaml` with `--frozen-lockfile`; catalog-pinned versions; mise tools SHA-256-checksummed per platform in `mise.lock` (Node, pnpm, Java, fakecloud, Bun); DynamoDB Local's archive SHA-256-checked on download; no `pull_request_target`; **now:** the CI token is `contents: read` and every action is pinned by commit (SR-7)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | SR-30                                  |
| **Abuse of the service** (spam, reputation)                                           | **D/E** using aliases, custom domains, temp mail or the outbox to send spam; burning the shared SES reputation                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | No open relay (above); replies only to contacts; drafts only to contacts, confirmed in the web app; since §20.33 new recipients only by a signed-in owner (compose) or as a contact they add (send-to address, mailed from their DKIM-verified mailbox), 20 a day, and 100 sends a day for replies, compose and drafts together, nothing to suppression-listed addresses; confirmations 10 a day; custom domains need DNS proof and SES DKIM; temp domains receive-only (§20.22.2); bounces suppress, complaints mark, reputation alarms (§20.13)                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | SR-9, SR-10, SR-13                     |

#### Fixed in this review

Code fixes come with regression tests that fail on `main`; the infra and CI changes are typechecked (`tsc -p infra`). `pnpm check` and `pnpm test:affected` pass on each step.

| ID   | Severity | Finding                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | Fix                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| ---- | -------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| SR-1 | High     | **Crafted mail stalls the inbound Lambda.** Quoted-printable decoding (`/[ \t]+(?=\r?\n\|$)/`), multipart splitting and tracker rewriting (a `reduce` copying its growing array per match), HTML-to-text (`<[^>]*>`, lazy `<style>…</style>` pairs without a close), CSS `url(`, the extractor's address blanking, line context and candidate grouping were quadratic; MIME nesting was unbounded. Measured: 80 k spaces in a QP part took 5 s, 40 k parts 2 s, 100 k `<p\t` 8.5 s; 1–2 MB of such mail held the Lambda for its whole 60 s, and the inbound Lambda has no reserved concurrency, so a flood also starved other functions. | `fix(core): process attacker-chosen mail in linear time`: lookbehinds so a match starts only where its run starts, tags end at the next `<`, paired elements searched only up to their last closing tag (`internal/regex.ts`), one `flatMap` instead of copying reduces, the MIME walk bounded to 16 levels and 1,000 parts (the rest passes unchanged and is not read), the extractor's context bounded to 200 characters. `packages/core/test/Adversarial.test.ts`: 16 crafted 2 MB inputs through forward, reply, read and extract, each under 5 s (well under a second locally); the corpora are unchanged. |
| SR-2 | High     | **Any user could delete the temp-mail domains' SES identities.** `domains.normalize` refused only the shared domains, so adding `tmp.example` as a custom domain succeeded, adopted the existing identity (`IdentityExists`), and removing the domain, or its 72-hour expiry in the cron, called `DeleteEmailIdentity`; IAM's `Deny` named only the shared domains. Temp mail would stop until a redeploy recreated the identity (possibly with new Easy DKIM tokens, so new DNS records).                                                                                                                                               | `fix(api): refuse temp-mail domains as custom domains`: temp domains, their subdomains and parents are refused like the shared ones (`shared-domain`: one of the service's own); the identity `Deny` covers them; sending from them is denied explicitly; `SendConfirmations` names this account instead of `*`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| SR-3 | Medium   | **Ambiguous reply senders passed as the owner.** The owner check read `commonHeaders.from[0]` only; `From: owner@x, mallory@y` or a second `From` field went out as the owner's reply (the stack test proves it on the emulator), while which address SES's DMARC verdict covers is undocumented (RFC 7489 §6.6.1 leaves it to the receiver).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | `fix(inbound): refuse ambiguous reply senders, …`: a reply needs exactly one `From` field holding one mailbox, the one SES listed (`Policy.rejectAmbiguousSender`, `Mail.isSingleMailbox`).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| SR-4 | Medium   | **Headers that leak or route around the alias.** Forwarded mail kept `Mail-Followup-To`/`Mail-Reply-To` (Thunderbird and mutt answer to them, so the owner's reply went from their real address to whoever the sender named) and `X-Confirm-Reading-To`; replies kept `Autocrypt` and `OpenPGP` (the owner's real address and key) and `Resent-*`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | Same commit: removed on both paths, respectively on replies.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| SR-5 | Medium   | **Client metadata documents were read without a cap.** Only `Content-Length` was checked, then the whole body read; a `client_id` URL answering chunked could stream into the 256 MB API Lambda for the 5 s timeout, on every authorization request.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | `fix(api): read client metadata documents with a byte cap`: streamed and refused past 5 KiB; a test host that never stops writing is cut off (it timed out before).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| SR-6 | Low      | **Stored text outside the MCP fence.** `create_alias` and `set_alias_active` put the alias address in the summary, before `<untrusted-mail-data>`; on-the-fly aliases have sender-chosen local parts.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | `fix(api): keep stored text out of MCP summaries`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| SR-7 | Low      | **Hardening.** `localhost.` and single labels with a trailing dot passed `WebhookUrl.check` (the connect-time guard still refused them); 3fff::/20 and 5f00::/16 were missing; three Lambdas kept logs forever; the spool bucket accepted plain HTTP; the table had no deletion protection; CI had no `permissions` block and used movable action tags.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | `fix(core): refuse localhost. …`, `fix(infra): log retention on every Lambda, …`, `ci: read-only token, actions pinned by commit`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |

#### Open findings, prioritized

"Beta" means a private beta with invited users; "launch" means open sign-up.

| ID    | Severity | Blocks    | Finding and evidence                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | Proposal / owner decision                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| ----- | -------- | --------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| SR-8  | Medium   | — (fixed) | **Fixed in §20.26.** ~~CloudFront isn't in infra, and the rate limits depend on it.~~ Every limit keyed on `x-alias-source-ip` = the function URL's `sourceIp`; behind CloudFront that is an edge address, and switching to `CloudFront-Viewer-Address` while the function URL stays `authType: NONE` would let anyone call the URL directly and pick their address. No security headers were set anywhere.                                                                                                                                                          | **Done (§20.26):** one distribution (app from private S3 with OAC, API with (a) an origin secret from SSM that the API checks in constant time before trusting `CloudFront-Viewer-Address`, refusing everything else with 403, image proxy with OAC); (b) security headers by a CloudFront Function (`default-src 'self'`, `style-src` with `'unsafe-inline'` for the inbox frame, `frame-ancestors 'none'`, `object-src`/`base-uri 'none'`, HSTS with `includeSubDomains`, `nosniff`, `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`), the browser tests run under them; (c) IPv6 keyed by /64; (d) WAF with IP reputation, known bad inputs and two rate rules, on the flat-rate Free plan ($0). Still needs a deploy to verify (§20.26's list). |
| SR-9  | Medium   | launch    | ✅ **Fixed (§20.27).** **Sign-up and WebAuthn challenges have no rate limit** (`apps/api/src/auth.ts` `registerOptions`, `register`, `loginOptions`). Attestation is `none`, so a script with a software authenticator registers in a loop: usernames and their subdomains squatted, and every per-user allowance (10 confirmation mails, 50 temp inboxes, 50 sends, 3 custom-domain claims a day) multiplied, against the shared SES reputation and the bill; each options call writes a challenge item.                                                            | **Done (§20.27):** per-address limits on challenges (30 an hour) and accounts (5 a day) with the `Quota` helper on `ClientSource`, a per-account limit on failed logins (10 an hour), a global hourly circuit breaker (`ALIAS_MAX_SIGNUPS_PER_HOUR`, 100), Cloudflare Turnstile before a registration challenge is issued (secret from SSM, verified emulator), RFC 2142 role names reserved as usernames and as local parts on user subdomains. Trustworthy addresses behind CloudFront still come with SR-8 (§20.26). **Owner:** the default numbers; invite codes for the beta in addition (not built); the Cloudflare widget and its secret before a deploy.                                                                   |
| SR-10 | Medium   | launch    | ✅ **Partially fixed (§20.28).** **One crafted message could stop a user's forwarding.** `Feedback.bounced` (`apps/inbound/src/Feedback.ts`) acts on any `Permanent` bounce: it puts every bounced address on the account-wide suppression list and marks the owner's recipient, after which the pipeline drops everything (`recipient-bounced`). A sender who gets the owner's provider to refuse one message permanently (content or policy rejections, 5.7.x, are often reported as `Permanent`/`General`) cuts the owner off until they resend the confirmation. | Treat only `bounceSubType` `General`/`NoEmail`/`Suppressed`/`OnAccountSuppressionList` with a 5.1.x (address) status as an address bounce; for forwards, mark the recipient only after two such bounces within a week, and keep 5.7.x as a per-message failure. Needs recorded bounces from a sandbox account (§20.3) to pick the exact fields. **Owner:** how aggressive to be with the shared reputation vs. one user's forwarding.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| SR-11 | Medium   | launch    | **The reply check accepts DMARC passes not proven by the owner's own DKIM.** `Policy.rejectReply` needs DKIM `PASS` (any domain's) and DMARC `PASS`, but DMARC can pass on SPF alignment alone; on shared outbound infrastructure that lets one customer pass SPF for another's domain (published for Microsoft 365), an attacker who also knows the reverse alias (only the owner's mailbox sees it) could reply as the owner.                                                                                                                                      | Also require an aligned DKIM pass: SES adds an `Authentication-Results` field (authserv-id `amazonses.com`) to received mail, which `Mail` already strips as our own verdicts, so read the topmost one with that authserv-id and require `header.i`/`header.d` to equal or be a parent of the `From` domain. The ses-emulator must write the same field, from a recorded message first (emulator rule), so it waits for a sandbox account.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| SR-12 | Medium   | — (fixed) | **Fixed (§20.29).** **The image proxy is easy to exhaust.** Anyone can get proxy URLs signed by mailing their own alias with `proxy-images` on (`Mail.forward` signs every remote image); ten reserved slots × a 5 s deadline is about 2 uncached fetches a second for everyone, and the proxy fetches from AWS addresses for the requester.                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | Done: URLs carry the alias owner, sealed per alias; 60 fetches per owner and 300 per host a minute, unreachable origins left alone for a minute, 2 s connect/first-byte deadlines, reserved concurrency 20. Not signed per reader (a shared URL spends the sharer's budget). Per-user WAF rules remain with SR-8.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| SR-13 | Low      | launch    | **Fixed (§20.30).** **Webhook signing secrets are stored in plain text** (`packages/db/src/WebhookRepo.ts`); everything else secret in the table is a hash. Anyone with table or backup read access can forge deliveries to users' receivers.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | **Decision proposed: yes, seal them like inbox bodies**, with a fourth SSM keyring `/alias/<stage>/webhook-keys` (AES-256-GCM, HKDF per key version, AAD = user id + webhook id), read by the API (create/rotate) and the delivery Lambda (sign). No new AWS service, so no new emulator; about one day of work with tests.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| SR-14 | Low      | launch    | **Fixed (§20.30).** **CSRF rests on `SameSite=Strict` alone.** Effect's HttpApi treats a body without `Content-Type` as JSON, so a cross-site `fetch(…, { mode: "no-cors", body: blob })` is a "simple" request with a JSON body; only the cookie's `SameSite` stops it. A same-site foothold (a future `img.<app domain>` serving user-influenced content) would turn that into CSRF on `POST /v1/outbox/:id/send`.                                                                                                                                                 | In `Authentication.session`, refuse non-GET requests whose `Sec-Fetch-Site` isn't `same-origin` (or whose `Origin` isn't the relying party's), and require `application/json` on JSON endpoints. Small; do it with SR-8, where the production origin exists.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| SR-15 | Low      | launch    | **Fixed (§20.30).** **The consent page trusts metadata documents' names.** For a metadata-document client the title is `Connect <client_name>?` and `client_uri` is shown unchecked; only DCR clients get "Name not verified" (`apps/web/src/routes/oauth.authorize.tsx`). A document on `claude-connect.example` can call itself "Claude". Error redirects also send the user to a URI the same attacker chose (`Authorize.ts`), one click away.                                                                                                                    | Title metadata-document clients by their host (`Connect claude-connect.example ("Claude")`), drop `client_uri` unless it is on the `client_id`'s host, and name the destination host on "Return to the app".                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| SR-16 | Low      | —         | **The prompt-injection fence is static.** `<` is escaped inside it, so no value can close it, but `structuredContent` is unfenced (clients may show it to the model) and a fixed marker can be imitated in prose.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | A per-response random marker suffix; say in every tool description that `structuredContent` is the same untrusted data. Confirm-gated send already caps the damage.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| SR-17 | Low      | —         | **Fixed (§20.30).** **Registered OAuth clients never expire** (`OAuthRepo`), 10 per address and hour, unbounded with IPv6.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | TTL of 30 days on a client until its first grant.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |

The web app's iframe CSP (`img-src data:`) blocks the load, but the API, SDK, CLI and MCP hand the HTML out as "sanitized".                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | Drop `style` attributes whose value contains `\`, `image-set`, `@import` or `expression`, or drop inline styles entirely for API reads.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| SR-19 | Low      | — (fixed) | **Fixed (§20.29).** **Contacts are created before the hourly cap** (`Pipeline.ts`: `contactFor` in planning, `reserveForward` in `admit`), so a flood with changing `From` addresses keeps writing contact items after it is rate-limited.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | Done: planning only reads the contact; it is created after the loop check and the hourly cap admitted the message.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| SR-20 | Low      | — (fixed) | **Fixed (§20.29).** **No reserved concurrency for inbound and feedback.** A flood on the public API URL can take the account's concurrency, SES's async invokes are throttled and retried for 6 hours, then lost.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | Done: reserved concurrency on all six functions (352 in all; the quota needs ≥ 452, ask for 1,000 before the first deploy), async failures of inbound and feedback recorded in an SQS queue after 6 h, throttle, backlog and failure alarms. Needs an account to verify.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| SR-21 | Low      | —         | **Reply leaks that remain.** `References`/`In-Reply-To` can hold the owner's own earlier `Message-ID` (naming their provider, not their address); attachments (vCards, `application/pgp-keys`, signatures) and HTML-entity-encoded addresses aren't scrubbed (§20.3).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | Rewrite our own earlier `Message-ID`s in `References`; document attachments as the owner's choice. A field allowlist instead of the current denylist would be the stronger form; it needs the reply corpus extended first.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| SR-22 | Info     | —         | **Display-name lookalikes.** `"PayPal (service@paypal.com)"` arrives as `PayPal (service@paypal.com) (attacker@x)`; the real address is always appended but may be cut off in narrow views. Inherent to forwarding.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | Optionally put the real address first.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| SR-23 | Low      | —         | **`identity/*` for `SendEmail`** (§20.18.2). Every identity in the account is the service's own, unverified ones can't send, the pipeline sends only from verified domains of the alias's owner, the shared and temp identities can't be mutated, and the temp ones can't send (SR-2).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | **Accept** for beta and launch. Narrowing needs identity tags and an `aws:ResourceTag` condition on `SendEmail`, unverified; try it on the sandbox account.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| SR-24 | Low      | —         | **A message is lost if the Lambda dies after its claim** (§20.6, by choice: at most once). With SR-1 fixed, attacker mail no longer causes it; a legitimate slow message still could.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | Keep; revisit with the two-phase claim §20.6 describes if logs show it.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| SR-25 | Info     | —         | **Partly fixed (§20.30):** session list, "sign out everywhere else", passkey list; step-up for send and device approval and an idle timeout remain. **Sessions.** 30-day absolute lifetime, no idle timeout, no session list or "sign out everywhere", no passkey list (§20.16). A stolen cookie works for 30 days. Erasure needs a fresh passkey assertion, but device approval (minting a 90-day token) and sending a draft need only the session.                                                                                                                 | A sessions/passkeys screen with revoke, and a fresh assertion for `POST /v1/outbox/:id/send` and device approval (the same step-up erasure has).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| SR-26 | Info     | —         | **Partly fixed (§20.30):** the conditional counter update; device misses and pending domain claims remain. **Smaller auth items.** The passkey counter is written without a `counter < :new` condition (most passkeys don't count); a crafted `/device?code=WRONG` link burns a victim's 10 lookups an hour; a pending custom-domain claim blocks others for 72 hours, renewable by re-claiming.                                                                                                                                                                     | Conditional counter update; count device misses per user and code; let a domain's DNS proof take a pending claim from another user.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| SR-27 | Info     | —         | **Key usage.** Inbox KEKs seal with random 96-bit IVs; NIST caps that at 2³² seals per key.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | Rotate the inbox keyring yearly or every 10⁸ messages, whichever is first (runbook, §20.22.2).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| SR-28 | Low      | —         | **Confused deputy left to defaults.** Alchemy's Scheduler role trusts `scheduler.amazonaws.com` without `aws:SourceAccount` (upstream); the SES events topic has the default policy, so any principal in the account allowed to publish could forge bounces and get addresses suppressed.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | An explicit topic policy (only `ses.amazonaws.com` with `aws:SourceAccount` + the configuration set's ARN); an upstream issue for Alchemy, or a hand-made role.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| SR-29 | Info     | —         | **Fixed table name in IAM ARNs** (§20.3): ARNs are built from `alias-<stage>`, the name Alchemy creates.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | **Accept**, as proposed: the name is part of the stage, and a rename would fail closed (denied), not open.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| SR-30 | Low      | —         | **Supply chain gaps.** `minimumReleaseAge` isn't set explicitly in `pnpm-workspace.yaml` (only its exclude list), so the delay is pnpm's default; Playwright's Chromium and the cached DynamoDB Local jar aren't hash-checked (TLS only, test-only); OpenPGP.js 6.x has no published audit (encrypt-only use, §20.21); Dependabot/Renovate isn't configured.                                                                                                                                                                                                         | Set `minimumReleaseAge: 1440` (one day) explicitly; re-hash the jar after a cache restore; Renovate with digest pinning for actions; watch OpenPGP.js advisories (the inbound Lambda is the only place it runs).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |

**Anonymous temp inboxes** (§3.5, not built): **decision proposed: don't build them before SR-8 is deployed and SR-9** (the client address CloudFront vouches for exists since §20.26) (SR-9's Turnstile client, emulator and per-address quotas exist since §20.27). They need a client address CloudFront can vouch for, a proof (Turnstile) and per-address quotas, and an anonymous token that only reads its own inbox. Until then temp inboxes stay behind an account (§20.22.2), which is also what keeps temp domains off blocklists longer.

**§20.3's legacy items, from a security angle.** Recorded AWS payloads (SR-11 and SR-10 depend on them); TTL lag (readers already ignore expired items); infra only typechecked (every IAM claim above is unverified until a deploy); the null-sender spelling (only affects bounce classification); spam always dropped (fails safe); reply scrubbing limits (SR-21); unrecorded SES/SNS events (SR-10); SSM IAM/KMS behaviour; the on-the-fly race during erasure (forwards nothing, accepted); Chromium-only browser tests; inbox storage IAM and lifecycle; the webhooks gaps (SR-13 decided above; the soft cap of ten endpoints, not atomic, is **accepted**: it is session-only, costs one SQS message per extra endpoint per event, and a race adds at most a few); device-login erasure residue (accepted, unusable); CloudFront routing (SR-8); tracker-protection limits (privacy, not safety); OpenPGP (SR-30). None is a hidden vulnerability; the ones that matter are numbered above.

#### Verdict

- **Private beta (invited users): yes, once CloudFront is deployed with SR-8's origin secret and headers** (built in §20.26; deploy and run its checks). The two High findings are fixed; nothing open lets an outsider send as an alias, read someone's mail or tokens, or reach internal addresses. Before inviting anyone, also verify on a real account what only AWS can show (below).
- **Public launch: not yet.** SR-9 (sign-up limits and a proof) is fixed (§20.27). It needs SR-8 deployed (§20.26: WAF, and the trustworthy client address SR-9's limits now count), SR-10's re-check on recorded bounces (§20.28), SR-11 (aligned DKIM, once recorded) (SR-12 and SR-20, capacity, are fixed in §20.29; SR-13, SR-14 and SR-15 in §20.30), plus an external penetration test and the recorded-payload work of phase 2.

**Needs a real AWS account to verify.** Every IAM statement and resource policy (incl. the `identity/*` `Deny` patterns, the temp-domain send `Deny`, `ses:PutSuppressedDestination`/`DeleteSuppressedDestination` on `Resource: "*"`, which as far as the docs say support no resource-level permissions); what SES writes for multiple `From` fields and in `Authentication-Results` (SR-3, SR-11); bounce subtypes and diagnostic codes (SR-10); CloudFront's `CloudFront-Viewer-Address`, origin headers, security-headers function and WAF (SR-8, the list in §20.26); concurrency quotas and the async failure queue (SR-20, §20.29); Lambda's IPv6 egress (whether 3fff::/20 etc. are reachable at all); Undici's `lookup` on Lambda's network; S3 lifecycle and TLS-only policies; deletion protection with Alchemy's update path.

### 20.26 CloudFront, WAF and a trustworthy client address (2026-10-23)

Fixes SR-8 (§20.25). The API, the web app and the image proxy now sit behind **one CloudFront distribution** with an AWS WAF web ACL, every response of the app's origin carries security headers, and the per-client rate limits count the viewer CloudFront saw. Before this, every limit keyed on the function URL's `sourceIp`, which behind CloudFront is an edge server: 20 bad tokens from one edge would have locked out everyone using it.

#### How the API knows a request came through CloudFront

**OAC can't protect the API.** CloudFront's Origin Access Control signs requests to a Lambda function URL with SigV4, and for `POST`/`PUT` Lambda then needs the body's SHA-256 in `x-amz-content-sha256`, computed by the viewer ("Restrict access to an AWS Lambda function URL origin"; Lambda takes no unsigned payloads). Browsers, MCP clients, the CLI and OAuth clients don't send it. OAC also replaces the viewer's `Authorization` header with its own signature, and the API's bearer tokens travel in it. So the API's function URL stays `authType: NONE`, and **an origin custom header proves the way in** instead:

- `/alias/<stage>/origin-secrets` is a fourth SSM keyring (`<version>:<base64url>`, the first one current; `OriginSecret` in `@alias/core`). The deploy reads it and puts the current key into the distribution's origin custom header `x-alias-origin-secret`; CloudFront overwrites a viewer-sent header of the same name ("Add custom headers to origin requests"). The API reads the keyring at cold start (`ALIAS_ORIGIN_SECRETS_PARAMETER`, `FrontConfig`) and accepts the current or a retired key, compared in constant time. Rotation takes three steps (append, put first and deploy, drop); `infra/alchemy.run.ts` spells them out.
- **A request without the proof is refused with 403 before any handler runs** (`FunctionUrl.handle` with `FunctionUrl.cloudFront(keys)`), even `/health`. Nobody can skip the WAF or pick their own address by calling the function URL directly. A proven request without a parseable `CloudFront-Viewer-Address` is refused too, and logged as an error: it means the origin request policy stopped forwarding it, and falling back to the edge address would bring the shared-limit problem back.
- **The client address** is then `CloudFront-Viewer-Address` (`198.51.100.10:46532`; IPv6 without brackets, so the port is whatever follows the last colon; brackets are accepted too; several comma-joined values are refused). `FunctionUrl` puts it into `x-alias-source-ip`, where handlers read it, and removes the origin-secret and viewer-address headers so no handler sees them. Called directly (`FunctionUrl.direct`: `pnpm dev`, tests), it is `sourceIp`, as before, and client-sent copies of all three headers are ignored.
- **IPv6 clients count per /64** (`ClientAddress.rateKey`), SR-8 (c): one subscriber or one cloud host gets a whole /64 and could otherwise take a new address for every attempt. IPv4-mapped IPv6 (`::ffff:192.0.2.1`) counts as the IPv4 address.

Why not the other options: `X-Forwarded-For` is appended to, never replaced, so its first entry is whatever the viewer sent; a CloudFront Function copying `event.viewer.ip` into a header of ours would work as well, but costs a function invocation on every API request and only moves the trust question to that header; mutual TLS to the origin needs the Business plan.

#### The distribution

| Behavior         | Origin                                      | Cache / origin request policy (all AWS managed)                  | Function                                                      |
| ---------------- | ------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------- |
| default (`/*`)   | S3 bucket `alias-web-<stage>`, private, OAC | `CachingOptimized` (honours each object's `Cache-Control`)       | viewer request: SPA routes; viewer response: security headers |
| `/v1/*`          | API function URL + origin secret            | `CachingDisabled` / `AllViewerExceptHostHeader`, all methods     | viewer response: security headers                             |
| `/mcp`           | same                                        | same                                                             | same                                                          |
| `/.well-known/*` | same                                        | same                                                             | same                                                          |
| `/img/*`         | image proxy function URL, `AWS_IAM` + OAC   | `CachingOptimized` (path-only key, no cookies or viewer headers) | none: the proxy sets its own headers                          |

- **Web app.** The build (`apps/web/dist`) is uploaded by Alchemy's `Website.AssetDeployment`: `assets/**` (hashed names) with `public, max-age=31536000, immutable`, everything else (`index.html`) with `no-cache`. The bucket policy allows `s3:GetObject` only to `cloudfront.amazonaws.com` for this distribution (`AWS:SourceArn`) and denies plain HTTP. A viewer-request CloudFront Function sends every path whose last segment has no `.` to `/index.html` (`/aliases`, `/device`, `/oauth/authorize`); custom error responses can't do that, because they would also replace the API's own 403 and 404 answers.
- **API.** `AllViewerExceptHostHeader` is the managed policy meant for function URLs (they expect their own `Host`); it forwards every viewer header, cookies and query strings, plus CloudFront's viewer location headers, `CloudFront-Viewer-Address` among them. `CloudFront-Viewer-Address` can only come from an origin request policy, never a cache policy. The origin waits 35 s (the API runs up to 30). §20.24's routing (`/mcp`, `/.well-known/*`, `/v1/*` to the API, `/oauth/authorize` from the app) is these behaviors.
- **Image proxy.** It moved from its own distribution into this one, under `/img/*` (the path its signed URLs always had); `ALIAS_IMAGE_PROXY_URL` is now the app's origin. One distribution because a flat-rate plan covers exactly one distribution and one apex domain, and because the proxy needs no domain of its own: its answers are raster images only, with `nosniff` and a `sandbox` CSP, and CloudFront forwards no cookies to it.
- **TLS.** An ACM certificate for the app domain in us-east-1 (Alchemy's `ACM.Certificate` default), DNS-validated. DNS stays on Cloudflare, and since §20.37 the stack does the rest in one deploy: it waits for ACM's validation record (a CNAME `_<hash>.<app domain>` → `_<hash>.<id>.acm-validations.aws.`), puts it into Cloudflare (DNS only, kept for renewals), waits until ACM reports `ISSUED` (CloudFront refuses a pending certificate), then adds the app domain as an alternate domain name with SNI and `TLSv1.2_2021` and points it at the distribution (a CNAME, flattened at the apex, DNS only).
